{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Qef6kqj6bCqXP7V1jYd5E1","type":"tool_result","content":"# Ticket-Triage Benchmark -- Scoring Specification\n\n| Field   | Value                |\n|---------|----------------------|\n| Version | v2.3                 |\n| Owner   | eval-platform team   |\n| Status  | Authoritative        |\n\nThis document defines how predictions from the ticket-triage benchmark are normalized, scored, and ranked. It covers every step from raw prediction files through the final leaderboard order.\n\nThis document is authoritative. Where `pipeline/scorer.py` and this document disagree, this document wins. Acceptance tests for the scorer should treat this document as the contract.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Inputs\n\n**Gold labels.** File `data/gold/gold_labels.jsonl`, one JSON object per line:\n\n```json\n{\"example_id\": \"string\", \"label\": \"string\"}\n```\n\nGold labels are always canonical taxonomy labels (never aliases, never pre-normalized).\n\n**Predictions.** Files `data/predictions/shard_00.jsonl` through `data/predictions/shard_11.jsonl`, one JSON object per line:\n\n```json\n{\"example_id\": \"string\", \"model\": \"string\", \"label\": \"string\"}\n```\n\n**Category weights.** Defined in `pipeline/config.py` (`CATEGORY_WEIGHTS`) and reproduced here as the canonical taxonomy table:\n\n| Category         | Weight |\n|------------------|--------|\n| account_access   | 3      |\n| billing          | 3      |\n| technical_error  | 3      |\n| product_defect   | 3      |\n| refunds          | 2      |\n| shipping         | 2      |\n| returns          | 2      |\n| data_privacy     | 2      |\n| subscription     | 2      |\n| warranty         | 1      |\n| feature_request  | 1      |\n| general_inquiry  | 1      |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Prediction normalization\n\nNormalization applies to **predicted labels only**. Gold labels are used as-is.\n\n**§2.1** Lowercase the label, strip leading and trailing whitespace, and collapse every internal run of whitespace to a single space. Whitespace means Unicode whitespace as recognized by Python's `str.split()` (so U+00A0 counts).\n\n**§2.2** Replace every underscore (`_`) and hyphen (`-`) with a space. This happens before whitespace collapsing, so `\"Sign-In\"` becomes `\"sign in\"` and `\"billing__issue\"` becomes `\"billing issue\"`.\n\n**§2.3** Alias mapping. After §2.1--§2.2, if the resulting string appears in the alias table below, replace it with the mapped canonical label. The match is exact, whole-string only. The mapping is applied at most once: the alias output is final and is never re-applied. Canonical labels that do not appear in the table pass through unchanged.\n\n### Alias table\n\n39 entries. Normalized form on the left, canonical label on the right.\n(The space-separated forms of taxonomy labels are listed as aliases so a\ncanonical label still maps to itself after separator normalization.)\n\n| Normalized form    | Canonical label  |\n|--------------------|------------------|\n| login issue        | account_access   |\n| sign in            | account_access   |\n| signin             | account_access   |\n| locked out         | account_access   |\n| payment            | billing          |\n| billing issue      | billing          |\n| invoice            | billing          |\n| overcharge         | billing          |\n| refund             | refunds          |\n| money back         | refunds          |\n| bug                | technical_error  |\n| crash              | technical_error  |\n| error              | technical_error  |\n| site down          | technical_error  |\n| defect             | product_defect   |\n| broken item        | product_defect   |\n| faulty             | product_defect   |\n| delivery           | shipping         |\n| package            | shipping         |\n| tracking           | shipping         |\n| return             | returns          |\n| exchange           | returns          |\n| privacy            | data_privacy     |\n| gdpr request       | data_privacy     |\n| delete my data     | data_privacy     |\n| cancel subscription| subscription     |\n| upgrade plan       | subscription     |\n| downgrade          | subscription     |\n| guarantee          | warranty         |\n| suggestion         | feature_request  |\n| feature idea       | feature_request  |\n| question           | general_inquiry  |\n| other              | general_inquiry  |\n| account access     | account_access   |\n| technical error    | technical_error  |\n| product defect     | product_defect   |\n| data privacy       | data_privacy     |\n| feature request    | feature_request  |\n| general inquiry    | general_inquiry  |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Validity and row handling\n\n**§3.1** After normalization (§2), if the label is not one of the 12 canonical taxonomy labels, the prediction is INVALID.\n\n**§3.2** If a gold example has no prediction row for a given model, that model's prediction for it is INVALID.\n\n**§3.3** If the same `example_id` appears more than once for one model, the **first** occurrence wins and later ones are ignored. Order is defined as: shard filename ascending (`shard_00` ... `shard_11`), then row order within the file. At the API level: the order of the `predictions` list as passed.\n\n**§3.4** A prediction row whose `example_id` does not exist in the gold set is ignored entirely. It contributes nothing: no false positive, no effect at all.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Counting\n\n**§4.1** For each gold example, exactly one of the following applies:\n\n- The final (normalized, aliased) predicted label **equals** the gold label: count one **TP** for that category.\n- The prediction is valid but **differs** from the gold label: count one **FP** for the predicted category AND one **FN** for the gold category.\n- The prediction is **INVALID** (§3.1 or §3.2): count one **FN** for the gold category only. INVALID predictions never produce an FP for any category.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Per-category F1\n\n**§5.1**\n\n```\nF1_c = 2 * TP_c / (2 * TP_c + FP_c + FN_c)\n```\n\nIf the denominator is 0, `F1_c = 0.0`.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Aggregation\n\n**§6.1** The per-category output includes **every** category in the weights table, even those with zero gold examples (report `tp=0, fp=0, fn=0, f1=0.0` for unseen categories). The overall aggregate, however, includes **only** categories with at least one gold example. The `weights` mapping passed to the scorer defines the reporting and aggregation universe; validity (§3.1) is always judged against the canonical 12-label taxonomy, regardless of `weights`.\n\n**§6.2** The overall score before rounding:\n\n```\noverall_unrounded = sum(w_c * F1_c) / sum(w_c)\n```\n\nBoth sums run over gold-present categories only. All intermediate values stay unrounded.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Rounding\n\n**§7.1** The final overall score is rounded to 4 decimal places using `ROUND_HALF_UP`.\n\nExample: `0.86665` rounds to `0.8667`.\n\n> **Warning.** Python's built-in `round()` uses banker's rounding and will produce `0.8666` for the value above. Do not use it. Use:\n>\n> ```python\n> from decimal import Decimal, ROUND_HALF_UP\n> Decimal(str(x)).quantize(Decimal(\"0.0001\"), rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP)\n> ```\n\nPer-category `f1` values in the output are **not** rounded.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Ranking\n\n**§8.1** Models are ranked by rounded overall score, descending. Ties are broken by model name, ascending lexicographic (ASCII) order.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Worked end-to-end example\n\n### Setup\n\nOnly two categories carry gold examples: billing (weight 3) and refunds (weight 2).\n\nGold set:\n\n| example_id | label   |\n|------------|---------|\n| e1         | billing |\n| e2         | billing |\n| e3         | refunds |\n| e4         | refunds |\n\n### Model predictions and normalization\n\n| example_id | raw label        | after §2.1--2.2    | after §2.3 (alias) | result         |\n|------------|------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------------|\n| e1         | `\" Billing \"`    | `\"billing\"`         | (canonical, no alias) | TP(billing)  |\n| e2         | `\"payment\"`      | `\"payment\"`         | `\"billing\"`         | TP(billing)    |\n| e3         | `\"refund\"`       | `\"refund\"`          | `\"refunds\"`         | TP(refunds)    |\n| e4         | `\"shipping issue\"`| `\"shipping issue\"` | not a taxonomy label, not an alias | INVALID, FN(refunds), no FP |\n\n### Counts and per-category F1\n\n| Category | TP | FP | FN | F1                          |\n|----------|----|----|----|------------------------------|\n| billing  | 2  | 0  | 0  | 4/4 = 1.0                   |\n| refunds  | 1  | 0  | 1  | 2/(2+0+1) = 2/3 = 0.6666... |\n\n### Overall score\n\n```\noverall = (3 * 1.0 + 2 * (2/3)) / (3 + 2)\n        = (3 + 4/3) / 5\n        = 13/15\n        = 0.8666666...\n```\n\nRounded with `ROUND_HALF_UP` to 4 decimal places: **0.8667**.\n\n### Micro-examples\n\n**(a) Duplicate handling (§3.3).** If `e1` appears twice for a model, first with label `\"billing\"` then with label `\"refunds\"`, the `\"billing\"` row is used.\n\n**(b) Separator chain (§2.1--2.2, §2.3).** `\"Sign-In\"` normalizes to `\"sign in\"` (§2.1--2.2), then maps via alias to `account_access` (§2.3).\n\n---\n\n## 10. Scorer API\n\nBoth functions live in `pipeline/scorer.py` and must be importable with these exact names and signatures.\n\n```python\ndef score_model(gold: dict[str, str], predictions: list[dict], weights: dict[str, float]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Score a single model's predictions against the gold set.\n\n    Args:\n        gold: example_id -> canonical label.\n        predictions: [{\"example_id\": str, \"label\": str}, ...] in canonical order (see §3.3).\n        weights: category -> weight.\n\n    Returns:\n        {\"per_category\": {cat: {\"tp\": int, \"fp\": int, \"fn\": int, \"f1\": float}},\n         \"overall\": float}\n\n    overall is rounded per §7.1. Per-category f1 values are unrounded.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\ndef rank_models(overall_scores: dict[str, float]) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Rank models for the leaderboard.\n\n    Args:\n        overall_scores: model name -> rounded overall score.\n\n    Returns:\n        Model names ranked per §8.1 (descending score, ascending name on ties).\n    \"\"\"\n```","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1f16dcb2-b78f-4d01-adbd-11f07f49b969","uuid":"92b4dc88-86cc-4786-8d34-1d480a7edcf5","timestamp":"2026-08-31T07:10:47.460Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# Ticket-Triage Benchmark -- Scoring Specification\n\n| Field   | Value                |\n|---------|----------------------|\n| Version | v2.3                 |\n| Owner   | eval-platform team   |\n| Status  | Authoritative        |\n\nThis document defines how predictions from the ticket-triage benchmark are normalized, scored, and ranked. It covers every step from raw prediction files through the final leaderboard order.\n\nThis document is authoritative. Where `pipeline/scorer.py` and this document disagree, this document wins. Acceptance tests for the scorer should treat this document as the contract.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Inputs\n\n**Gold labels.** File `data/gold/gold_labels.jsonl`, one JSON object per line:\n\n```json\n{\"example_id\": \"string\", \"label\": \"string\"}\n```\n\nGold labels are always canonical taxonomy labels (never aliases, never pre-normalized).\n\n**Predictions.** Files `data/predictions/shard_00.jsonl` through `data/predictions/shard_11.jsonl`, one JSON object per line:\n\n```json\n{\"example_id\": \"string\", \"model\": \"string\", \"label\": \"string\"}\n```\n\n**Category weights.** Defined in `pipeline/config.py` (`CATEGORY_WEIGHTS`) and reproduced here as the canonical taxonomy table:\n\n| Category         | Weight |\n|------------------|--------|\n| account_access   | 3      |\n| billing          | 3      |\n| technical_error  | 3      |\n| product_defect   | 3      |\n| refunds          | 2      |\n| shipping         | 2      |\n| returns          | 2      |\n| data_privacy     | 2      |\n| subscription     | 2      |\n| warranty         | 1      |\n| feature_request  | 1      |\n| general_inquiry  | 1      |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Prediction normalization\n\nNormalization applies to **predicted labels only**. Gold labels are used as-is.\n\n**§2.1** Lowercase the label, strip leading and trailing whitespace, and collapse every internal run of whitespace to a single space. Whitespace means Unicode whitespace as recognized by Python's `str.split()` (so U+00A0 counts).\n\n**§2.2** Replace every underscore (`_`) and hyphen (`-`) with a space. This happens before whitespace collapsing, so `\"Sign-In\"` becomes `\"sign in\"` and `\"billing__issue\"` becomes `\"billing issue\"`.\n\n**§2.3** Alias mapping. After §2.1--§2.2, if the resulting string appears in the alias table below, replace it with the mapped canonical label. The match is exact, whole-string only. The mapping is applied at most once: the alias output is final and is never re-applied. Canonical labels that do not appear in the table pass through unchanged.\n\n### Alias table\n\n39 entries. Normalized form on the left, canonical label on the right.\n(The space-separated forms of taxonomy labels are listed as aliases so a\ncanonical label still maps to itself after separator normalization.)\n\n| Normalized form    | Canonical label  |\n|--------------------|------------------|\n| login issue        | account_access   |\n| sign in            | account_access   |\n| signin             | account_access   |\n| locked out         | account_access   |\n| payment            | billing          |\n| billing issue      | billing          |\n| invoice            | billing          |\n| overcharge         | billing          |\n| refund             | refunds          |\n| money back         | refunds          |\n| bug                | technical_error  |\n| crash              | technical_error  |\n| error              | technical_error  |\n| site down          | technical_error  |\n| defect             | product_defect   |\n| broken item        | product_defect   |\n| faulty             | product_defect   |\n| delivery           | shipping         |\n| package            | shipping         |\n| tracking           | shipping         |\n| return             | returns          |\n| exchange           | returns          |\n| privacy            | data_privacy     |\n| gdpr request       | data_privacy     |\n| delete my data     | data_privacy     |\n| cancel subscription| subscription     |\n| upgrade plan       | subscription     |\n| downgrade          | subscription     |\n| guarantee          | warranty         |\n| suggestion         | feature_request  |\n| feature idea       | feature_request  |\n| question           | general_inquiry  |\n| other              | general_inquiry  |\n| account access     | account_access   |\n| technical error    | technical_error  |\n| product defect     | product_defect   |\n| data privacy       | data_privacy     |\n| feature request    | feature_request  |\n| general inquiry    | general_inquiry  |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Validity and row handling\n\n**§3.1** After normalization (§2), if the label is not one of the 12 canonical taxonomy labels, the prediction is INVALID.\n\n**§3.2** If a gold example has no prediction row for a given model, that model's prediction for it is INVALID.\n\n**§3.3** If the same `example_id` appears more than once for one model, the **first** occurrence wins and later ones are ignored. Order is defined as: shard filename ascending (`shard_00` ... `shard_11`), then row order within the file. At the API level: the order of the `predictions` list as passed.\n\n**§3.4** A prediction row whose `example_id` does not exist in the gold set is ignored entirely. It contributes nothing: no false positive, no effect at all.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Counting\n\n**§4.1** For each gold example, exactly one of the following applies:\n\n- The final (normalized, aliased) predicted label **equals** the gold label: count one **TP** for that category.\n- The prediction is valid but **differs** from the gold label: count one **FP** for the predicted category AND one **FN** for the gold category.\n- The prediction is **INVALID** (§3.1 or §3.2): count one **FN** for the gold category only. INVALID predictions never produce an FP for any category.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Per-category F1\n\n**§5.1**\n\n```\nF1_c = 2 * TP_c / (2 * TP_c + FP_c + FN_c)\n```\n\nIf the denominator is 0, `F1_c = 0.0`.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Aggregation\n\n**§6.1** The per-category output includes **every** category in the weights table, even those with zero gold examples (report `tp=0, fp=0, fn=0, f1=0.0` for unseen categories). The overall aggregate, however, includes **only** categories with at least one gold example. The `weights` mapping passed to the scorer defines the reporting and aggregation universe; validity (§3.1) is always judged against the canonical 12-label taxonomy, regardless of `weights`.\n\n**§6.2** The overall score before rounding:\n\n```\noverall_unrounded = sum(w_c * F1_c) / sum(w_c)\n```\n\nBoth sums run over gold-present categories only. All intermediate values stay unrounded.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Rounding\n\n**§7.1** The final overall score is rounded to 4 decimal places using `ROUND_HALF_UP`.\n\nExample: `0.86665` rounds to `0.8667`.\n\n> **Warning.** Python's built-in `round()` uses banker's rounding and will produce `0.8666` for the value above. Do not use it. Use:\n>\n> ```python\n> from decimal import Decimal, ROUND_HALF_UP\n> Decimal(str(x)).quantize(Decimal(\"0.0001\"), rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP)\n> ```\n\nPer-category `f1` values in the output are **not** rounded.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Ranking\n\n**§8.1** Models are ranked by rounded overall score, descending. Ties are broken by model name, ascending lexicographic (ASCII) order.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Worked end-to-end example\n\n### Setup\n\nOnly two categories carry gold examples: billing (weight 3) and refunds (weight 2).\n\nGold set:\n\n| example_id | label   |\n|------------|---------|\n| e1         | billing |\n| e2         | billing |\n| e3         | refunds |\n| e4         | refunds |\n\n### Model predictions and normalization\n\n| example_id | raw label        | after §2.1--2.2    | after §2.3 (alias) | result         |\n|------------|------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------------|\n| e1         | `\" Billing \"`    | `\"billing\"`         | (canonical, no alias) | TP(billing)  |\n| e2         | `\"payment\"`      | `\"payment\"`         | `\"billing\"`         | TP(billing)    |\n| e3         | `\"refund\"`       | `\"refund\"`          | `\"refunds\"`         | TP(refunds)    |\n| e4         | `\"shipping issue\"`| `\"shipping issue\"` | not a taxonomy label, not an alias | INVALID, FN(refunds), no FP |\n\n### Counts and per-category F1\n\n| Category | TP | FP | FN | F1                          |\n|----------|----|----|----|------------------------------|\n| billing  | 2  | 0  | 0  | 4/4 = 1.0                   |\n| refunds  | 1  | 0  | 1  | 2/(2+0+1) = 2/3 = 0.6666... |\n\n### Overall score\n\n```\noverall = (3 * 1.0 + 2 * (2/3)) / (3 + 2)\n        = (3 + 4/3) / 5\n        = 13/15\n        = 0.8666666...\n```\n\nRounded with `ROUND_HALF_UP` to 4 decimal places: **0.8667**.\n\n### Micro-examples\n\n**(a) Duplicate handling (§3.3).** If `e1` appears twice for a model, first with label `\"billing\"` then with label `\"refunds\"`, the `\"billing\"` row is used.\n\n**(b) Separator chain (§2.1--2.2, §2.3).** `\"Sign-In\"` normalizes to `\"sign in\"` (§2.1--2.2), then maps via alias to `account_access` (§2.3).\n\n---\n\n## 10. Scorer API\n\nBoth functions live in `pipeline/scorer.py` and must be importable with these exact names and signatures.\n\n```python\ndef score_model(gold: dict[str, str], predictions: list[dict], weights: dict[str, float]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Score a single model's predictions against the gold set.\n\n    Args:\n        gold: example_id -> canonical label.\n        predictions: [{\"example_id\": str, \"label\": str}, ...] in canonical order (see §3.3).\n        weights: category -> weight.\n\n    Returns:\n        {\"per_category\": {cat: {\"tp\": int, \"fp\": int, \"fn\": int, \"f1\": float}},\n         \"overall\": float}\n\n    overall is rounded per §7.1. Per-category f1 values are unrounded.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\ndef rank_models(overall_scores: dict[str, float]) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Rank models for the leaderboard.\n\n    Args:\n        overall_scores: model name -> rounded overall score.\n\n    Returns:\n        Model names ranked per §8.1 (descending score, ascending name on ties).\n    \"\"\"\n```","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

/app/workspace/pipeline/run_scoring.py ===\n\"\"\"Score all prediction shards against gold and write per-example rows.\n\nUsage:\n    python -m pipeline.run_scoring            # fresh full run (rebuilds the DB)\n    python -m pipeline.run_scoring --resume   # continue an interrupted run\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport sys\n\nfrom pipeline import checkpoint\nfrom pipeline.config import (\n    CATEGORY_WEIGHTS,\n    GOLD_PATH,\n    PREDICTIONS_DIR,\n    SHARDS,\n    shard_of,\n)\nfrom pipeline.data_io import connect, insert_rows, read_jsonl, reset_db\nfrom pipeline.normalize import normalize_label\n\n\ndef load_gold():\n    return {row[\"example_id\"]: row[\"label\"] for row in read_jsonl(GOLD_PATH)}\n\n\ndef score_shard(shard, gold):\n    \"\"\"Build result rows for one shard. Returns list of DB tuples.\"\"\"\n    rows_by_model = {}\n    for row in read_jsonl(PREDICTIONS_DIR / f\"{shard}.jsonl\"):\n        eid = row[\"example_id\"]\n        if eid not in gold:\n            continue  # stray id: contributes nothing (SPEC 3.4)\n        if shard_of(eid) != shard:\n            continue\n        per_model = rows_by_model.setdefault(row[\"model\"], {})\n        if eid in per_model:\n            continue  # duplicate: first occurrence wins (SPEC 3.3)\n        per_model[eid] = row[\"label\"]\n\n    shard_gold = {eid: g for eid, g in gold.items() if shard_of(eid) == shard}\n    models = sorted(rows_by_model)\n    out = []\n    for model in models:\n        preds = rows_by_model[model]\n        for eid in sorted(shard_gold):\n            raw = preds.get(eid)\n            if raw is None:\n                final = None  # missing prediction: INVALID (SPEC 3.2)\n            else:\n                final = normalize_label(raw)\n                if final not in CATEGORY_WEIGHTS:\n                    final = None  # non-taxonomy label: INVALID (SPEC 3.1)\n            out.append((model, eid, shard, shard_gold[eid], raw, final))\n    return out\n\n\ndef run(resume=False, log=print):\n    gold = load_gold()\n    state = checkpoint.load()\n    conn = connect()\n    if resume:\n        plan = checkpoint.resume_plan(state, SHARDS)\n        log(f\"resume: {len(state['completed'])} shards recorded done\")\n    else:\n        reset_db(conn)\n        state = {\"completed\": [], \"in_progress\": None}\n        checkpoint.save(state)\n        plan = list(SHARDS)\n        log(f\"fresh run: scoring {len(plan)} shards\")\n    for shard in plan:\n        checkpoint.set_in_progress(state, shard)\n        rows = score_shard(shard, gold)\n        insert_rows(conn, rows)\n        checkpoint.mark_done(state, shard)\n        log(f\"{shard}: committed {len(rows)} rows\")\n    conn.close()\n    log(\"scoring complete\")\n\n\ndef main(argv=None):\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--resume\", action=\"store_true\")\n    args = ap.parse_args(argv)\n    run(resume=args.resume)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n=== /app/workspace/pipeline/scorer.py ===\n\"\"\"Scoring math for the ticket-triage benchmark.\n\nImplements the contract in docs/SPEC.md (which is authoritative). The two\npublic entry points are score_model() and rank_models(); the small helpers\nare shared with build_leaderboard so DB aggregation uses identical math.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom decimal import Decimal, ROUND_HALF_UP\n\nfrom pipeline.config import CATEGORY_WEIGHTS\nfrom pipeline.normalize import normalize_label\n\n\ndef f1_from_counts(tp: int, fp: int, fn: int) -> float:\n    denom = 2 * tp + fp + fn\n    return (2 * tp / denom) if denom else 0.0  # SPEC 5.1\n\n\ndef round_overall(x: float) -> float:\n    # SPEC 7.1. Built-in round() is banker's rounding; do not use it here.\n    return float(Decimal(str(x)).quantize(Decimal(\"0.0001\"), rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP))\n\n\ndef overall_from_category_f1(per_category_f1: dict, gold_present: set, weights: dict) -> float:\n    num = sum(weights[c] * per_category_f1[c] for c in weights if c in gold_present)\n    den = sum(weights[c] for c in weights if c in gold_present)  # SPEC 6.1/6.2\n    return round_overall(num / den) if den else 0.0\n\n\ndef score_model(gold: dict, predictions: list, weights: dict) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"See docs/SPEC.md section 10 for the full contract.\"\"\"\n    seen = {}\n    for row in predictions:\n        eid = row[\"example_id\"]\n        if eid not in gold:\n            continue  # SPEC 3.4\n        if eid in seen:\n            continue  # SPEC 3.3: first occurrence wins\n        seen[eid] = row[\"label\"]\n\n    counts = {c: {\"tp\": 0, \"fp\": 0, \"fn\": 0} for c in weights}\n    for eid, gold_label in gold.items():\n        final = normalize_label(seen[eid]) if eid in seen else None\n        valid = final in CATEGORY_WEIGHTS if final is not None else False\n        if valid and final == gold_label:\n            counts[gold_label][\"tp\"] += 1\n        elif valid:\n            if final in counts:\n                counts[final][\"fp\"] += 1\n            counts[gold_label][\"fn\"] += 1\n        else:\n            counts[gold_label][\"fn\"] += 1  # INVALID: FN only (SPEC 4.1)\n\n    per_category = {\n        c: {**counts[c], \"f1\": f1_from_counts(**counts[c])} for c in weights\n    }\n    overall = overall_from_category_f1(\n        {c: per_category[c][\"f1\"] for c in weights}, set(gold.values()), weights\n    )\n    return {\"per_category\": per_category, \"overall\": overall}\n\n\ndef rank_models(overall_scores: dict) -> list:\n    # SPEC 8.1: descending score; ties broken by name ascending.\n    return [m for m, _ in sorted(overall_scores.items(), key=lambda kv: (-kv[1], kv[0]))]","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1f16dcb2-b78f-4d01-adbd-11f07f49b969","uuid":"8697b36a-cd08-482e-8444-c34539fcb653","timestamp":"2026-08-31T07:10:56.247Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"=== shards have committed to the results DB so\nan interrupted monthly run can resume instead of starting over.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\n\nfrom pipeline.config import CHECKPOINT_PATH\n\n\ndef load():\n    try:\n        with open(CHECKPOINT_PATH, \"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n            return json.load(f)\n    except FileNotFoundError:\n        return {\"completed\": [], \"in_progress\": None}\n\n\ndef save(state):\n    CHECKPOINT_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with open(CHECKPOINT_PATH, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n        json.dump(state, f, indent=2, sort_keys=True)\n        f.write(\"\\n\")\n\n\ndef set_in_progress(state, shard):\n    state[\"in_progress\"] = shard\n    save(state)\n\n\ndef mark_done(state, shard):\n    if shard not in pass after an interrupted run.\"\"\"\n    completed = list(state[\"completed\"])\n    stuck = state.get(\"in_progress\")\n    if stuck:\n        # The interrupted shard was mid-write when the run died; sqlite rolls\n        # the open transaction back on reconnect, and that rollback can also\n        # clip the tail of the previously committed batch in the same WAL\n        # segment. The two\npublic entry points are score_model() and rank_models(); the small helpers\nare shared with build_leaderboard so DB aggregation uses identical math.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom decimal import Decimal, ROUND_HALF_UP\n\nfrom pipeline.config import CATEGORY_WEIGHTS\nfrom pipeline.normalize import normalize_label\n\n\ndef f1_from_counts(tp: int, fp: int, fn: int) -> float:\n    denom = 2 * tp + fp + fn\n    return (2 * tp / denom) if denom else 0.0  # SPEC 5.1\n\n\ndef round_overall(x: float) -> float:\n    # SPEC 7.1. Built-in round() is banker's rounding; do not use it here.\n    return float(Decimal(str(x)).quantize(Decimal(\"0.0001\"), rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP))\n\n\ndef overall_from_category_f1(per_category_f1: dict, gold_present: set, weights: dict) -> float:\n    num = sum(weights[c] * per_category_f1[c] for c in weights if c in gold_present)\n    den = sum(weights[c] for c in weights if c in gold_present)  # SPEC 6.1/6.2\n    return round_overall(num / den) if den else 0.0\n\n\ndef score_model(gold: dict, predictions: list, weights: dict) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"See docs/SPEC.md section 10 for the full contract.\"\"\"\n    seen = {}\n    for row in predictions:\n        eid = row[\"example_id\"]\n        if eid not in gold:\n            continue  # SPEC 3.4\n        if eid in seen:\n            continue  # SPEC 3.3: first occurrence wins\n        seen[eid] = row[\"label\"]\n\n    counts = {c: {\"tp\": 0, \"fp\": 0, \"fn\": 0} for c in weights}\n    for eid, gold_label in gold.items():\n        final = normalize_label(seen[eid]) if eid in seen else None\n        valid = final in CATEGORY_WEIGHTS if final is not None else False\n        if valid and final == gold_label:\n            counts[gold_label][\"tp\"] += 1\n        elif valid:\n            if final in counts:\n                counts[final][\"fp\"] += 1\n            counts[gold_label][\"fn\"] += 1\n        else:\n            counts[gold_label][\"fn\"] += 1  # INVALID: FN only (SPEC 4.1)\n\n    per_category = {\n        c: {**counts[c], \"f1\": f1_from_counts(**counts[c])} for c in weights\n    }\n    overall = overall_from_category_f1(\n        {c: per_category[c][\"f1\"] for c in weights}, set(gold.values()), weights\n    )\n    return {\"per_category\": per_category, \"overall\": overall}\n\n\ndef rank_models(overall_scores: dict) -> list:\n    # SPEC 8.1: descending score;
